=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integers A, B, and M.
How many permutations P = (P_1, \dots, P_{AB-1}) of (1, 2, \ldots, AB - 1) satisfy all of the following conditions? Find the count modulo M.

- The length of a longest increasing subsequence of P is A.
- The length of a longest decreasing subsequence of P is B.
- There exists an integer n such that appending n + 0.5 to the end of P does not change either of the lengths of a longest increasing subsequence and a longest decreasing subsequence.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
A B M

Output

Print the number of permutations satisfying the conditions, modulo M.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 2 \leq A, B
- AB \leq 120
- 10^8 \leq M \leq 10^9
- M is a prime.

Sample Input 1

3 2 998244353

Sample Output 1

10

For example, P = (2, 4, 5, 1, 3) satisfies the conditions. This can be confirmed as follows:

- The length of a longest increasing subsequence of P is 3.
- The length of a longest decreasing subsequence of P is 2.
- For n = 4, the lengths of longest increasing and decreasing subsequences of (2, 4, 5, 1, 3, 4.5) are 3 and 2, respectively.

There are 10 permutations of (1, 2, 3, 4, 5) that satisfy the conditions.

Sample Input 2

10 12 924844033

Sample Output 2

623378361

Print the count modulo M.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's recompute Q for reverse (3,1,5,4,2) very carefully using row insertion and recording new cells.

RSK algorithm:
Initialize P empty, Q empty.
For k=1..n:
  Insert x=w_k into P using row bumping. The new cell created in P (where the inserted number eventually rests) is at some position (i,j).
  Put k into Q at that same position.

Let's do reverse w^r=(3,1,5,4,2):

k=1, x=3:
P row1 empty -> place 3 at (1,1). New cell (1,1). Q[1,1]=1.
P: [3]
Q: [1]

k=2, x=1:
Insert 1 into row1: row1 [3], 1<3, so 3 is bumped. Now insert 3 into row2: row2 empty, place 3 at (2,1). New cell (2,1). Q[2,1]=2.
P: row1 [1], row2 [3]
Q: row1 [1], row2 [2]

k=3, x=5:
Insert 5 into row1: row1 [1], 5>1, so place 5 at (1,2). No bump. New cell (1,2). Q[1,2]=3.
P: row1 [1,5], row2 [3]
Q: row1 [1,3], row2 [2]

k=4, x=4:
Insert 4 into row1: row1 [1,5], 4<5, so 5 bumped. Insert 5 into row2: row2 [3], 5>3, so place 5 at (2,2). New cell (2,2). Q[2,2]=4.
P: row1 [1,4], row2 [3,5]
Q: row1 [1,3], row2 [2,4]

k=5, x=2:
Insert 2 into row1: row1 [1,4], 2<4, so 4 bumped. Insert 4 into row2: row2 [3,5], 4<5, so 5 bumped. Insert 5 into row3: row3 empty, place 5 at (3,1). New cell (3,1). Q[3,1]=5.
P: row1 [1,2], row2 [3,4], row3 [5]
Q: row1 [1,3], row2 [2,4], row3 [5]

So Q(rev)=[[1,3],[2,4],[5]]. Yes.

Q(w)^t for original w=(2,4,5,1,3): original Q we computed [[1,2,3],[4,5]]? Let's recompute original Q carefully:
w=(2,4,5,1,3)
k=1 x=2: P [2], Q[1,1]=1.

- Let T_0 be T with n removed (a SYT of shape μ = λ minus corner c).

Maybe we can count pairs (P,Q) satisfying U + evac(Q)_{B,1} > N+1. Since evac is a bijection on SYT of shape λ, we can replace R = evac(Q). Then condition is U + R_{B,1} > N+1, where P and R are independent SYT of shape λ? Since for each Q there is exactly one R=evac(Q), and evac is an involution/bijection. The pair (P,Q) is equivalent to (P,R) with P,R independent SYT of shape λ. Because Q and R are in bijection. Great! Then the condition depends only on P and R:
- U = P_{1,A} (last entry of first row of P).
- R_{B,1} (last entry of first column of R).
And P, R are independent SYT of shape λ.

Thus the count is:
Number of pairs (P,R) of SYT of shape λ such that P_{1,A} + R_{B,1} > N+1.
Since P and R independent, we can count by summing over possible values x = P_{1,A}, y = R_{B,1}:
Count = Σ_{x+y > N+1} f_x * f_y,
where f_x = number of SYT of shape λ with last entry of first row = x, and f_y = number of SYT of shape λ with last entry of first column = y.

But note the set of possible x and y have symmetry: The last entry of first row in a SYT and last entry of first column. Since shape λ = (A^{B-1}, A-1). There may be a simple formula for f_x and f_y. Alternative: Since N<=120 and number of ideals of the rectangle is at most ~646k, we can compute s directly by dynamic programming over self-evacuating tableaux using the fact that a self-evacuating tableau is determined by its first half? Let's explore.

A self-evacuating tableau T of shape λ has the property that evacuation is identity. There is a known characterization: T is self-evacuating iff for every k, the shape of entries ≤ k and the shape of entries ≥ N+1-k are complementary in some sense. Specifically, the chain of partitions of T is "self-complementary": λ^k = (λ \ λ^{N-k})^*? If we can use this, we can count self-evacuating tableaux by DP over ideals up to half.

Let's derive the self-evacuation condition in terms of the chain of shapes.

Let T be a SYT of shape λ. Let λ^k be the shape of cells with entries ≤ k. This is a chain ∅=λ^0 ⊂ λ^1 ⊂ ... ⊂ λ^N=λ. The evacuation T^* has chain μ^k. There is a known relation:
μ^k = (λ \ λ^{N-k})^*  (1)
where * denotes 180-degree rotation of the complement within the bounding rectangle? Let's test with T3 shape (3,2) and evac=T1. For T3, λ^1=(1), λ^2=(2), λ^3=(2,1), λ^4=(2,2), λ^5=(3,2). For T1, μ^1=(1), μ^2=(2), μ^3=(3), μ^4=(3,1), μ^5=(3,2).
Take k=1: λ^{4}=(2,2). Complement in λ=(3,2) is cells not in (2,2): cell (1,3). Rotate 180 within λ? The complement shape is (1) (row1 col3). Rotate 180 gives (1)? μ^1=(1). Good.
k=2: λ^{3}=(2,1). Complement in λ: cells row1 col3, row2 col2? Wait λ cells: row1 1,2,3; row2 1,2. λ^3=(2,1) has row1 cols1,2; row2 col1. Remaining: row1 col3, row2 col2. As a skew shape, rotate 180: cells (1,3)->(2,1)? (2,2)->(1,2)? The rotated shape is (2)? μ^2=(2). Good.
k=3: λ^{2}=(2). Complement: row1 col3, row2 col1,2. Rotate 180 gives shape (3)? μ^3=(3). Good.
k=4: λ^{1}=(1). Complement: row1 col2,3; row2 col1,2. Rotate 180 gives shape (3,1)? μ^4=(3,1). Yes. So relation holds! Great.

Thus evacuation is characterized by the chain of shapes:
For all k, shape of entries ≤ k in T^* is (λ \ λ^{N-k})^*, where the complement is taken within λ and rotated 180 degrees (which yields a partition because λ is a Young diagram; for arbitrary λ, the complement rotated may not be a partition? Actually for any λ, the 180-degree rotation of the complement inside the bounding rectangle is a partition? It is the "complement" in the rectangle, yes if λ is a partition, the complement rotated is a partition? I think yes for the full bounding rectangle, but here complement within λ? Wait λ \ λ^{N-k} is a skew shape; rotating 180 and translating to fit gives a partition? For a Young diagram λ, the set of cells of λ not in a subshape μ, rotated 180, is a partition (the "skew" complement). Yes.

Self-evacuating T = T^* means for all k:
λ^k = (λ \ λ^{N-k})^*.
Equivalently, the chain of shapes is symmetric: λ^k determines λ^{N-k}. This is a strong condition.

This can be used to count self-evacuating tableaux by DP over shapes up to N/2. Since N<=120, number of shapes (partitions inside λ) is at most number of ideals ~646k. We can count chains λ^0⊂...⊂λ^N satisfying symmetry. But maybe simpler: A self-evacuating tableau is determined by the first half of the chain (shapes for k=1..floor(N/2)), and the second half is forced by symmetry, with consistency at the middle. We can count the number of such chains, which is exactly s.

Let's formalize.

Let λ be our shape. For any subshape μ ⊆ λ, define μ^* = (λ \ μ)^* (180-degree rotation of complement within λ). This is an involution on the set of subshapes of λ? If μ is a partition (order ideal), then μ^* is also an order ideal. It reverses inclusion: if μ⊆ν then μ^* ⊇ ν^*? Actually complement reverses, rotation preserves, so μ^* ⊇ ν^*.

The self-evacuation condition for T:
For each k, λ^k = (λ^{N-k})^*? Wait relation μ^k = (λ \ λ^{N-k})^* for T^*=T gives λ^k = (λ \ λ^{N-k})^*. Let ν = λ^{N-k}. Then λ^k = (λ \ ν)^* = ν^*? Since ν^* = (λ \ ν)^* by definition. Yes λ^k = (λ^{N-k})^*. So the chain is symmetric under *:
λ^k = (λ^{N-k})^* for all k.

Since * is an involution, this implies λ^{N-k} = (λ^k)^*.

Thus the sequence of shapes is determined by λ^0,...,λ^{floor(N/2)}. At each step, λ^k is obtained from λ^{k-1} by adding one cell. The forced shapes for k>N/2 are the stars of the earlier shapes, but they must form a valid chain (adding one cell at each step) and be consistent.

Specifically, for k from 0 to N:
λ^{N-k} = (λ^k)^*.
For k < N/2, this pairs shapes. The step from λ^{N-k-1} to λ^{N-k} corresponds under * to a step from λ^{k+1} to λ^k? Let's derive.

Let k < N/2. We have λ^{N-k} = (λ^k)^*, and λ^{N-k-1} = (λ^{k+1})^* (since N-(k+1)=N-k-1). Since λ^{N-k-1} ⊂ λ^{N-k}, applying * (which reverses inclusion) gives (λ^{N-k})^* ⊂ (λ^{N-k-1})^*, i.e. λ^k ⊂ λ^{k+1}. This is already true. The cell added at step N-k is the star of the cell added at step k+1? More precisely, the difference λ^{N-k} \ λ^{N-k-1} = (λ^k)^* \ (λ^{k+1})^* = (λ^{k+1} \ λ^k)^* (since complement/rotation). So the cell added at the paired position is determined by the cell added at step k+1.

Thus a self-evacuating tableau is determined by the first ceil(N/2) shapes? Let's handle parity.

Removing the two cells from row i leaves row i length l_i-2, and row i+1 length l_i-1, so l_i-2 < l_{i+1}, invalid. So need l_i-2 ≥ l_{i+1}. Thus horizontal domino removable iff l_i - 2 ≥ l_{i+1} (or i=B).
2. Two cells in the same column j at rows i and i+1, provided l_i = l_{i+1} = j? Actually vertical domino at the bottom of column j: cells (i,j) and (i+1,j). For removal to leave partition, need l_{i+1}=j and l_i=j? If l_i > j, then cell (i,j) is not on the boundary? It has right cell, but border strip can include cells not on boundary? A border strip must be connected and have no 2x2; it can include cells with right neighbors? For a vertical domino, if l_i > j, then cell (i,j) has a right neighbor, but the strip is just two cells; the right neighbor is not in the strip, so no 2x2. Is it removable? Removing cells (i,j) and (i+1,j) from a partition: row i length becomes j-1 (if l_i=j? Wait if l_i > j, removing cell (i,j) leaves a hole, not a partition unless all cells to the right are also removed? You cannot remove an interior cell. So for removal to leave a Young diagram, the removed cells must be at the ends of rows. Thus in a vertical domino, the top cell (i,j) must be the last cell of row i? Actually if l_i > j, removing (i,j) leaves row i with a gap at column j, invalid. Therefore top cell must be at the end of its row: l_i = j. Similarly bottom cell at end of row i+1: l_{i+1}=j. So vertical domino removable iff l_i = l_{i+1} = j, and after removal both rows reduce by1, need l_{i-1} ≥ j-1? Since l_{i-1} ≥ l_i = j, so yes; and l_{i+1} ≥ l_{i+2}? after reduction l_{i+1}-1 = j-1, need ≥ l_{i+2}; if l_{i+2}=j then invalid? Actually if l_{i+2}=j, then after removal row i+1 length j-1 < l_{i+2}=j, invalid. So need l_{i+1} > l_{i+2} (or i+1=B). Thus vertical domino at the bottom of a column where two consecutive rows have equal length j and the lower row is the last row of that length.

But wait border strips of length2 can also be a "hook" of two cells at a corner? A corner cell plus the cell above or left. That's exactly horizontal or vertical domino at a corner. Our characterization covers.

Generic function to enumerate border strips of length k for a partition:
A border strip (ribbon) of length k can be found by tracing the outer boundary. For k=2, we can enumerate:
- Horizontal domino in row i at columns l_i-1, l_i, if l_i >=2 and (i==B or l_i - 2 >= l_{i+1}). Because after reducing row i by2, row i length l_i-2 must be ≥ next row length. Also the left cell (i,l_i-1) must be on the boundary? If l_i-1 > l_{i+1}, yes; if l_i-1 = l_{i+1}, then left cell has a cell below, but the horizontal domino still has no 2x2? However after removal invalid as above. The condition l_i-2 ≥ l_{i+1} ensures l_i-1 > l_{i+1}? If l_i-2 ≥ l_{i+1}, then l_i-1 > l_{i+1}. So left cell is on boundary. Good.
- Vertical domino in column j at rows i,i+1, if l_i = l_{i+1} = j and (i+1==B or l_{i+1} > l_{i+2}). Because after reducing rows i and i+1 by1, need row i+1 length j-1 ≥ row i+2 length. Since l_{i+2} ≤ j; if l_{i+2}=j, then invalid. So need l_{i+1} > l_{i+2} (or last). Also the top cell (i,j) is at end of row i (l_i=j), so removable. The bottom cell at end of row i+1. The strip height = 2, sign = (-1)^{2-1} = -1.
For horizontal domino, height = 1, sign = +.

For k=1, border strips are single removable corners: cells (i,l_i) with (i==B or l_i > l_{i+1}). Sign +.

For our MN recursion, cycle lengths are all 2 except possibly one 1. We can process 1-cycles first or last. Since 1-cycle removal is sum over corners, if we process 1 first, then remaining cycles all 2. But the class w_0 has one 1-cycle if N odd. The order of cycles in MN doesn't matter. To simplify, we can define a recursive function solve(part, twos, ones) where ones is 0 or1. If ones=1, remove a corner (border strip length1) and recurse with ones=0. If twos>0, remove a 2-border strip and recurse with twos-1. Since all 2-cycles are identical, we don't need a list.

Base: if part empty and twos=0 and ones=0 return 1. If twos=0 and ones=1, sum over corners. If ones=0 and twos>0, sum over 2-border strips.

But note if ones=1 and twos>0, we can process ones first; MN rule allows any order. So:
def char(part, twos, ones):
    if empty: return 1 if twos==0 and ones==0 else 0
    if ones:
       return sum(char(part without corner, twos, 0) for corner in corners)
    if twos:
       return sum(sign * char(part without strip, twos-1, 0) for strip in 2-border strips)
Need memoization on (part tuple, twos, ones). Since ones only at start maybe, but recursion after removing ones has ones=0. We can just include ones.

Number of states: subshapes of λ times twos (0..m). twos up to 60. But for a given partition, twos is determined by (N - size(part) - ones)/2? Since we always remove cycles of length2 or1; the remaining cycles count is determined by the size of part: size(part) = 2*twos + ones. So twos and ones are determined by size and parity. Specifically if ones=1, size is odd; if ones=0, size even. Thus we can just use (part, ones) or (part, twos) with twos = (size - ones)//2. Memoize on (part, ones). Since size decreases by 2 or1. Good.

Implementation:
def char(part, ones):
    # part is tuple of row lengths (positive, nonincreasing)
    n = sum(part)
    if n == 0:
        return 1 if ones == 0 else 0
    # The remaining cycle type is determined by n and ones? Actually if ones is 1, the remaining cycles are one 1 and (n-1)//2 twos. If ones is 0, n even, cycles are n//2 twos.
    # But we can just branch based on ones.
    if ones == 1:
        # remove a corner (1-cycle)
        total = 0
        for i, l in enumerate(part):
            if i == len(part)-1 or l > part[i+1]:
                new_part = list(part)
                if l == 1:
                    new_part.pop(i)
                else:
                    new_part[i] -= 1
                # After decrement, need ensure nonincreasing; since l > part[i+1] and l-1 >= part[i+1]? If l > part[i+1], l-1 >= part[i+1] yes. If i>0, part[i-1] >= l > l-1. So valid.
                total += char(tuple(new_part), 0)
        return total
    else:
        # n even, remove a 2-border strip
        total = 0
        # horizontal dominoes
        for i, l in enumerate(part):
            if l >= 2:
                if i == len(part)-1 or l - 2 >= part[i+1]:
                    new_part = list(part)
                    if l == 2:
                        new_part.pop(i)
                    else:
                        new_part[i] -= 2
                    # Need ensure nonincreasing: if l-2 >= part[i+1] ok; if i>0, part[i-1] >= l > l-2.
                    total += char(tuple(new_part), 0)  # sign + for horizontal
        # vertical dominoes
        for i in range(len(part)-1):
            l = part[i]
            if part[i+1] == l:
                # top cell at end of row i, bottom at end of row i+1
                # need bottom row is last of this length: i+1 == len(part)-1 or part[i+1] > part[i+2]
                if i+1 == len(part)-1 or part[i+1] > part[i+2]:
                    new_part = list(part)
                    # remove cell (i,l) and (i+1,l), so both rows decrease by1
                    if l == 1:
                        # removing vertical domino in a column of height? If l=1, rows length1, removing both cells removes both rows? But l=1 and part[i+1]=1, after decrement both become0, pop both.
                        # Need handle carefully.
                        new_part.pop(i+1)
                        new_part.pop(i)
                    else:
                        new_part[i] -= 1
                        new_part[i+1] -= 1
                        # If row i+1 becomes 0? Since l=1 handled; if l>1, row i+1 becomes l-1 >0.
                        # Need ensure nonincreasing: new_part[i] = l-1, new_part[i+1] = l-1, and if i+2 exists, part[i+2] <= l-1 because part[i+1] > part[i+2] => l > part[i+2] => l-1 >= part[i+2]. Good.
                    total -= char(tuple(new_part), 0)  # sign - for vertical
        return total

Wait for vertical domino when l=1: part has consecutive rows of length1. Removing both cells removes both rows. Example part=(1,1,1), vertical domino rows2-3: l=1, i=1, part[2]=1, i+1 is last. new_part pop row3 and row2 -> (1). Good. If i+1 not last but part[i+1] > part[i+2] with l=1 impossible since part[i+2]>=1, so l > part[i+2] false. So l=1 vertical only at bottom. Good.

For horizontal domino when l=2 and pop row i: Need ensure if i>0, part[i-1] >= ? new row length0, but if i>0 and there are rows above, popping row i may violate? Example part=(2,2), horizontal domino in row2 l=2, i=1 last, pop row2 -> (2) valid. Horizontal in row1 l=2, i=0, condition l-2=0 >= part[1]=2 false, so not. If part=(2,1), horizontal in row1 l=2, condition 0>=1 false. So only bottom rows with no lower row or lower row length <=0. If pop row i not last? Condition l-2 >= part[i+1]; if l=2, need part[i+1] <=0 impossible, so only last. Good.

For vertical domino when l>1 and row i+1 becomes l-1, row i becomes l-1. If row i+1 was last, fine. If not, condition l > part[i+2] ensures l-1 >= part[i+2]. Good. If i>0, row i-1 >= l > l-1. Good.

Need also consider border strips of length2 that are neither horizontal nor vertical? For length2, any ribbon of two cells is either horizontal or vertical (since connected and no 2x2). So covered.

For sample A=3,B=2, s=1.

Now compute f^λ modulo M. Hook-length formula:
N = A*B - 1.
For each cell (i,j) in λ (1-indexed rows 1..B, row i length = A if i<B else A-1), hook length h = (λ_i - j) + (λ'_j - i) + 1. Need λ'_j = number of rows with length ≥ j. Since λ has B rows, last row length A-1. For j=1..A-1, λ'_j = B. For j=A, λ'_j = B-1.
So for row i:
- If i < B: λ_i = A.
  For j=1..A: h = (A-j) + (λ'_j - i) + 1.
  For j<=A-1: λ'_j=B => h = A-j + B-i +1 = A+B - i - j +1.
  For j=A: λ'_A=B-1 => h = 0 + (B-1 - i) +1 = B - i.
- If i = B: λ_B = A-1.
  For j=1..A-1: λ'_j=B => h = (A-1 - j) + (B - B) +1 = A - j.
So hook lengths:
For i=1..B-1:
  j=1..A-1: h = A+B - i - j +1.
  j=A: h = B - i.
For i=B:
  j=1..A-1: h = A - j.
We can compute product modulo M. Since M prime > N, factorial invertible. f = N! * inv(product) mod M. Need precompute factorials up to N and modular inverses maybe pow(product, M-2, M) once.

Now answer = f * (f - s) * inv2 mod M. Since s computed modulo M. If f-s negative, mod.

Need ensure s computed as integer modulo M. char returns modulo M. Since s is nonnegative but MN may produce negative mod. f - s mod M fine.

But we can also compute s using a direct DP over partitions by size? Let's see.

The MN recursion for cycles all 2 (and maybe one 1) can be interpreted as counting signed domino tilings of the partition by border strips. We can do DP over subshapes:
Let dp[part] = χ^{part}(2^k) for size=2k. For ones=1, s = sum over corners of dp[part without corner] where part initial? Actually if N odd, s = χ^λ(2^m,1). We can compute dp for all subshapes of even size, then sum corners. But recursion is fine.

Need ensure char with ones=1 first removes a corner, then calls char(...,0) for even size. So we only need char_even for subshapes. We can define:
def chi_even(part): # part size even, compute χ^part(2^{size/2})
    if size==0: return 1
    total = 0
    for horizontal strip: total += chi_even(part\strip)
    for vertical strip: total -= chi_even(part\strip)
    return total mod M
Then s = if N odd: sum over corners of chi_even(part without corner) mod M; if N even: chi_even(λ) (since w_0 all 2-cycles). Wait if N even, class is all 2-cycles, s=χ^λ(2^{N/2}) = chi_even(λ). If N odd, class is one 1-cycle plus 2-cycles, s=χ^λ(2^m,1) = sum over corners of chi_even(λ\corner). This is simpler. We don't need ones flag in recursion.

So implement chi_even(part) with memo. It only receives partitions of even size. It removes 2-border strips. For initial:
- If N even: s = chi_even(λ).
- If N odd: s = sum over corners c of chi_even(λ \ c) mod M.

This matches MN rule (process 1-cycle first). Good.

N=3 λ=(2,1): corners: (1,2)->(1,1), chi_even((1,1)) = -1 (vertical domino sign -); (2,1)->(2), chi_even((2)) = +1 (horizontal domino sign +); sum=0. Good.

Need enumerate 2-border strips for a partition. Let's write a helper that returns list of (new_part, sign) or directly adds.

Pseudo:

def chi_even(part):
    if not part: return 1
    if part in memo: return memo[part]
    total = 0
    m = len(part)
    # horizontal dominoes
    for i, l in enumerate(part):
        if l >= 2 and (i == m-1 or l - 2 >= part[i+1]):
            new = list(part)
            if l == 2:
                new.pop(i)
            else:
                new[i] -= 2
            total += chi_even(tuple(new))
    # vertical dominoes
    for i in range(m-1):
        l = part[i]
        if part[i+1] == l:
            if i+1 == m-1 or part[i+1] > part[i+2]:
                new = list(part)
                if l == 1:
                    new.pop(i+1)
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 1
                    new[i+1] -= 1
                    # If new[i+1] == 0? l=1 handled; if l>1, positive.
                total -= chi_even(tuple(new))
    memo[part] = total % MOD
    return memo[part]

MOD = M.
fact = 1
for i in 1..N: fact = fact * i % MOD
prod_hooks = 1
for i in 1..B:
  if i < B:
    for j in 1..A:
      if j < A:
        h = A + B - i - j + 1
      else: # j == A
        h = B - i
  else:
    for j in 1..A-1:
      h = A - j
  prod_hooks = prod_hooks * h % MOD
f = fact * pow(prod_hooks, MOD-2, MOD) % MOD

Now, final code structure:

import sys
sys.setrecursionlimit(1000000)
MOD = M

def chi_even(part, memo):
    if not part:
        return 1
    if part in memo:
        return memo[part]
    total = 0
    m = len(part)
    # horizontal
    for i, l in enumerate(part):
        if l >= 2 and (i == m-1 or l - 2 >= part[i+1]):
            new = list(part)
            if l == 2:
                new.pop(i)
            else:
                new[i] -= 2
            total += chi_even(tuple(new), memo)
    # vertical
    for i in range(m-1):
        l = part[i]
        if part[i+1] == l:
            if i+1 == m-1 or part[i+1] > part[i+2]:
                new = list(part)
                if l == 1:
                    new.pop(i+1)
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 1
                    new[i+1] -= 1
                total -= chi_even(tuple(new), memo)
    total %= MOD
    memo[part] = total
    return total

But note for vertical domino when l==1 and i+1 is last, new.pop(i+1); new.pop(i). If i=0 and part=(1,1), new becomes empty. Good. If part=(1,1,1), i=1, new after pop index2 then index1 -> (1). Good.

However, for vertical domino when l>1, after decrement, if new[i+1] becomes 0? l=1 handled. If l=1 and i+1 not last impossible due condition. Good.

Need compute corners for initial λ:
def corners(part):
    res=[]
    m=len(part)
    for i,l in enumerate(part):
        if i==m-1 or l > part[i+1]:
            new=list(part)
            if l==1:
                new.pop(i)
            else:
                new[i]-=1
            res.append(tuple(new))
    return res

def get_corners(part):
    m=len(part)
    res=[]
    for i,l in enumerate(part):
        if i==m-1 or l > part[i+1]:
            new=list(part)
            if l==1:
                new.pop(i)
            else:
                new[i]-=1
            # After decrement, if i>0 and new[i] < new[i+1]? But l > part[i+1] ensures l-1 >= part[i+1]. If l=1 and i not last impossible. So valid.
            res.append(tuple(new))
    return res

import sys
def main():
    A,B,M = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
    N = A*B - 1
    # initial partition
    part = (A,)*(B-1) + (A-1,)
    memo = {}
    def chi_even(p):
        if not p:
            return 1
        if p in memo:
            return memo[p]
        m = len(p)
        total = 0
        # horizontal
        for i,l in enumerate(p):
            if l >= 2 and (i == m-1 or l - 2 >= p[i+1]):
                new = list(p)
                if l == 2:
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 2
                total += chi_even(tuple(new))
        # vertical
        for i in range(m-1):
            l = p[i]
            if p[i+1] == l:
                if i+1 == m-1 or p[i+1] > p[i+2]:
                    new = list(p)
                    if l == 1:
                        new.pop(i+1)
                        new.pop(i)
                    else:
                        new[i] -= 1
                        new[i+1] -= 1
                    total -= chi_even(tuple(new))
        memo[p] = total
        return total

    if N % 2 == 0:
        chi = chi_even(part)
    else:
        chi = 0
        m = len(part)
        for i,l in enumerate(part):
            if i == m-1 or l > part[i+1]:
                new = list(part)
                if l == 1:
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 1
                chi += chi_even(tuple(new))
    s = abs(chi)

    # f_mod
    fact = 1
    for i in range(1, N+1):
        fact = fact * i % M
    prod = 1
    for i in range(1, B+1):
        if i < B:
            for j in range(1, A+1):
                if j < A:
                    h = A + B - i - j + 1
                else:
                    h = B - i
        else:
            for j in range(1, A):
                h = A - j
        prod = prod * h % M
    f = fact * pow(prod, M-2, M) % M
    s_mod = s % M
    ans = f * ((f - s_mod) % M) % M
    ans = ans * ((M + 1) // 2) % M
    print(ans)

Good. part=(1,1,1), corner i=2 l=1 last -> pop -> (1,1). Good. If l>1, decrement; if l-1 == 0? l=1 handled. If i not last and l > part[i+1], then l-1 >= part[i+1], so valid. If i last, no lower row.

Let's include sys.setrecursionlimit(1000000).

Potential issue: The chi_even recursion may revisit the same partition with different remaining number of 2-cycles? But remaining 2-cycles is determined by size/2. Since we always remove 2-cycles until empty, for a given partition size even, the remaining cycles are exactly size/2. So memo on part alone is valid. For N odd, we first remove a 1-cycle, then call chi_even on even-size partitions. Good.

Now, let's think if the MN rule for χ^λ(2^m) requires removing border strips of length 2 from the partition, but our border strip enumeration for length 2 might miss a border strip that is a "hook" of length2 at a corner? A hook of length2 is either horizontal or vertical. Covered.

Let's test with a shape where a border strip length2 is not at the end of a row/column? Border strips are always at the boundary. For length2, the two cells must be adjacent and one of them is a removable corner. If horizontal, the right cell is a corner; if vertical, the bottom cell is a corner. Our enumeration requires horizontal domino at end of row i with l_i-2 >= l_{i+1}, so the right cell (i,l_i) is a corner (no cell below because l_i > l_{i+1}? Actually l_i-2 >= l_{i+1} implies l_i > l_{i+1}, so right cell has no below; and no right because end of row. So corner). The left cell is adjacent and on boundary. Vertical similarly bottom cell is corner. Good.

Now, let's consider if a horizontal domino in row i with l_i-2 >= l_{i+1} but the left cell (i,l_i-1) has a cell below if l_i-1 = l_{i+1}? But l_i-2 >= l_{i+1} => l_i-1 > l_{i+1}, so no. Good.

Now, final answer code.

Let's write final code cleanly.

One more thought: The answer formula f*(f-s)/2 uses s = number of self-evacuating tableaux. We compute s = abs(χ). Is abs(χ) always equal to s for any partition? I think yes, but let's verify with shape (2,2) χ=2, s=2. Shape (3,1) χ=-1, s=1. Shape (1,1,1) χ=-1, s=1. Shape (2,1,1)? n=4, maybe χ? Not needed. I'm comfortable.

Let's maybe include a comment in code:
# s = number of fixed points of evacuation = |chi_lambda(w0)|.
# chi_lambda(w0) is computed by Murnaghan-Nakayama for cycle type 2^{N/2} (or 2^{(N-1)/2},1).

Now, final.
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

sys.setrecursionlimit(1_000_000)


def main():
    A, B, M = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
    N = A * B - 1

    # Shape lambda = (A^(B-1), A-1)
    start = (A,) * (B - 1) + (A - 1,)

    # chi_even(p) = irreducible character chi^p(2,2,...,2),
    # where |p| is even and the number of 2-cycles is |p|/2.
    memo = {}

    def chi_even(p):
        if not p:
            return 1
        if p in memo:
            return memo[p]

        m = len(p)
        total = 0

        # Remove a horizontal border strip of length 2.
        for i, l in enumerate(p):
            if l >= 2 and (i == m - 1 or l - 2 >= p[i + 1]):
                new = list(p)
                if l == 2:
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 2
                total += chi_even(tuple(new))

        # Remove a vertical border strip of length 2.
        for i in range(m - 1):
            l = p[i]
            if p[i + 1] == l:
                if i + 1 == m - 1 or p[i + 1] > p[i + 2]:
                    new = list(p)
                    if l == 1:
                        new.pop(i + 1)
                        new.pop(i)
                    else:
                        new[i] -= 1
                        new[i + 1] -= 1
                    total -= chi_even(tuple(new))

        memo[p] = total
        return total

    # chi^lambda(w_0), where w_0 is the longest permutation.
    if N % 2 == 0:
        chi = chi_even(start)
    else:
        # Cycle type is (2,2,...,2,1).
        chi = 0
        m = len(start)
        for i, l in enumerate(start):
            if i == m - 1 or l > start[i + 1]:
                new = list(start)
                if l == 1:
                    new.pop(i)
                else:
                    new[i] -= 1
                chi += chi_even(tuple(new))

    # Number of self-evacuating tableaux of this shape.
    s = abs(chi)

    # f = number of SYT of shape lambda, by hook-length formula.
    fact = 1
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        fact = fact * i % M

    hook_prod = 1
    for i in range(1, B + 1):
        if i < B:
            for j in range(1, A + 1):
                if j < A:
                    h = A + B - i - j + 1
                else:
                    h = B - i
        else:
            for j in range(1, A):
                h = A - j
        hook_prod = hook_prod * h % M

    f = fact * pow(hook_prod, M - 2, M) % M

    ans = f * ((f - s) % M) % M
    ans = ans * ((M + 1) // 2) % M
    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
